<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed

</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability

  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation

</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

    22	[![LinkedIn](https://img.shields.io/badge/-LinkedIn-black.svg?logo=linkedin&colorB=555)](https://www.linkedin.com/in/jackdewinter/)
    23	
    24	PyMarkdown is primarily a Markdown Linter.  To ensure that the Markdown
    25	[linting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lint_%28software%29)
    26	is accomplished successfully, the rules engine that powers the linter
    27	uses a Markdown parser that is both
    28	[GitHub Flavored Markdown](https://github.github.com/gfm/)
    29	compliant and
    30	[CommonMark](https://spec.commonmark.org/)
    31	compliant.  The rules provided in the base application can be easily extended
    32	by writing new plugins and importing them into the rules engine through simple
    33	configuration options.
    34	
    35	The PyMarkdown project has the following advantages:
    36	
    37	- Consistency
    38	  - This project can examine multiple files and directories with one invocation,
    39	    ensuring that all detected Markdown files adhere to the same guidelines.
    40	- Portable
    41	  - The linter runs on any system running Python 3.8 or later, with no modifications.
    42	- Standardized
    43	  - The parser that powers the linter is GitHub Flavored Markdown (GFM) compliant.
    44	    Due to that foundation, the parser does not guess how some parsers may handle
    45	    a given situation, as it has a clear set of rules to follow.
    46	- Accurate
    47	  - The parser passes all GFM conformance tests and CommonMark conformance tests.  In
    48	    test scenarios that were not present in either set of tests, the CommonMark
    49	    0.29.2 release was used to determine the correct parsing.
    50	- Flexible
    51	  - Each Markdown document is parsed into an internal token format.  Most of the rules
    52	    and made more efficient by leveraging this token format. Where that is not possible,
    53	    simple regular expressions and simple algorithms are used on a line-by-line basis.
    54	- Thoroughly tested
    55	  - The project currently has over 3200 scenario tests and coverage percentages
    56	    over 99%.
    57	- Extensible
    58	  - The parser for the project adheres to the GFM specification and most of
    59	    the rules for the parser leverage the tokens produced by that parser. The
    60	    [rules](/docs/rules.md) themselves are plugins, so they are extensible by default.
    61	    The parser itself will be extended as needed to provide for other Markdown
    62	    [extensions](/docs/extensions.md) as needed.
    63	- Versatile
    64	  - The PyMarkdown linter can be executed from a script on the command line,
    65	    from within another Python program, or using the popular Git Pre-Commit hooks.
    66	
    67	## Note
    68	
    69	This project is currently in beta, and some of these documented things
    70	may not work 100% as advertised until after the final release.  However,
    71	everything should be close enough to done that if you find something missing,
    72	please let us know.
    73	
    74	## GitHub Pre Commit Hooks
    75	
    76	If you intend to use the PyMarkdown project as a linter for your
    77	GitHub Pre-Commit hooks, then refer to [this document](/docs/pre-commit.md)
    78	on how to set that up.  Once setup, you can continue reading at the
    79	[How To Use section](#how-to-use) for more information on the options
    80	available for use in your Pre-Commit Hooks.

    87	
    88	```text
    89	pip install pymarkdownlnt

    92	## How To Use
    93	
    94	### If You Get Stuck
    95	
    96	Full help support is available by entering
    97	
    98	```shell
    99	pymarkdown --help
   100	```
   101	
   102	on the command line and pressing enter.  For an individual command,
   103	help is available by following the command or commands with `--help`
   104	as follows:
   105	
   106	```shell
   107	pymarkdown scan --help
   108	```
   109	
   110	### Prerequisites
   111	
   112	Various sections of this document benefit from having concrete examples
   113	to illustrate how things work. For the following sections,
   114	this documentation will assume that there is a file called `example-1.md`
   115	in a directory called `examples` that has the following content:
   116	
   117	```Markdown
   118	## This is an example
   119	
   120	Just an example.
   121	```
   122	
   123	and a file called `example-2.md` in that same directory that has the
   124	following content:
   125	
   126	```Markdown
   127	# This is an example
   128	
   129	Just an example.
   130	
   131	```
   132	
   133	If you prefer concrete files, these files are checked into the
   134	[examples directory](https://github.com/jackdewinter/pymarkdown/tree/main/examples) of the GitHub project.
   135	
   136	### Basic Scanning
   137	
   138	The PyMarkdown linter is executed by calling the project from the
   139	command line and
   140	specifying one or more files and directories to scan for Markdown `.md`
   141	files.  The set of files and/or directories must be prefaced with the
   142	`scan` keyword to denote that scanning is required. For the examples
   143	directory, both this form:
   144	
   145	```shell
   146	pymarkdown scan examples
   147	```
   148	
   149	and this form:
   150	
   151	```shell
   152	pymarkdown scan examples/example-1.md examples/example-2.md
   153	```
   154	
   155	can be used to scan both files in the directory.  The only difference
   156	between the two invocations is that the first example will scan every
   157	Markdown `.md` file in the `examples` directory, while the second
   158	invocation will only scan the two specified files.  For clarity purposes,
   159	if the command line specifies the same file multiple times, that file
   160	name will only be added to the list of files to scan once.
   161	
   162	If everything is working properly, both of the above scans will
   163	produce the following output:
   164	
   165	```text
   166	examples/example-1.md:3:16: MD047: Each file should end with a single newline character. (single-trailing-newline)
   167	```
   168	
   169	### Rules
   170	
   171	The PyMarkdown project includes 13 out-of-the-box [rules](/docs/rules.md),
   172	with another 29 rules to be added before the
   173	1.0.0 release.  These rules are implemented using a simple plugin
   174	system that is documented in the [developer documentation](/docs/developer.md).
   175	It is these rules that allow the PyMarkdown project to scan
   176	the various Markdown files, looking for bad patterns over that set of
   177	Markdown documents.
   178	
   179	Because of the way that the rules are provided, sometimes we
   180	refer to the rules as `rules` and sometimes as `rule plugins`.  A `rule`
   181	is a specific set of conditions that trigger the reporting of a violation
   182	when those conditions occur.  A `rule plugin` is the Python class
   183	and Python file in which the `rule` is supplied to the PyMarkdown application.
   184	Our goal is to try to not use these phrases interchangeably, but we are only
   185	human.  If we do mess up and use the wrong phase, we do apologize.
   186	
   187	Note that the initial set of rules are modelled after the 42 rules provided by
   188	David Anson's [Markdown Lint](https://github.com/markdownlint/markdownlint)
   189	project.  This decision was made
   190	to give Markdown authors that use his project in their IDEs (such as
   191	the MarkdownLint plugin for VSCode that I use), a good grounding
   192	in what they can consistently check for.
   193	
   194	### Rule Violation Format
   195	
   196	Executing either of the above example command lines will produce the following output:
   197	
   198	```text
   199	/examples/example-1.md:3:16: MD047: Each file should end with a single newline character. (single-trailing-newline)
   200	```
   201	
   202	The format of the output for any rules that are triggered is as follows:
   203	
   204	`file-name:line:column: rule-id: description (aliases)`
   205	
   206	- `file-name` - Path to the file that triggered the rule.
   207	- `line`/`column` - Position in the file where the rule was triggered.
   208	- `rule-id` - Unique identifier assigned to the rule.
   209	- `description` - Human readable description of the rule.
   210	- `aliases` - One or more aliases used to reference the rule.
   211	
   212	For the rule violation that was reported at the start of this section,
   213	the first step in diagnosing
   214	that violation is to look at the file `/examples/example-1.md` at the end of
   215	line 3, which is column 16.  Rule [md047](/docs/rules/rule_md047.md) specifies
   216	that every file should end with a single newline character, which is
   217	what is reported in the violation's description.  Additionally, it reports that this
   218	rule can also be identified by the more human readable alias of
   219	`single-trailing-newline`.
   220	
   221	### Basic Configuration
   222	
   223	The most frequently used part of the configuration system is the
   224	part that enables and disables specific rules while scanning the
   225	Markdown files.  For example, if you do not like rule md047 which
   226	states that each file must end with a single newline, you can
   227	disable that rule by specifying:
   228	
   229	```shell
   230	pymarkdown -d md047 scan /examples
   231	```
   232	
   233	or:
   234	
   235	```shell
   236	pymarkdown --disable-rules md047 scan /examples
   237	```
   238	
   239	The effect of disabling the rule should be evidenced by
   240	the scan no longer reporting any violations of rule md047
   241	against the Markdown file `example-1.md`.
   242	
   243	Alternatively, rules can also be enabled.  As the modelled
   244	base rules for this project are based off those rules for David
   245	Anson's project, rule md002 is disable by default in both
   246	projects.  Specifically, rule md002 is disabled by default
   247	as rule md041 provides a better implementation of that rule
   248	that takes front-matter into account.  Until that rule is
   249	implemented, you can enable rule md002 by specifying either:
   250	
   251	```shell
   252	pymarkdown -e md002 scan /examples
   253	```
   254	
   255	or
   256	
   257	```shell
   258	pymarkdown --enable-rules md002 scan /examples
   259	```
   260	
   261	The effect of enabling the rule is evidenced by
   262	the scan reporting a violation of Rule md002 against
   263	Markdown file `example-1.md`:
   264	
   265	```text
   266	examples/example-1.md:1:1: MD002: First heading of the document should be a top level heading.
   267	 [Expected: h1; Actual: h2] (first-heading-h1, first-header-h1)
   268	examples/example-1.md:3:16: MD047: Each file should end with a single newline character.
   269	(single-trailing-newline)
   270	```
   271	
   272	### Extensions
   273	
   274	Extensions are any features that are implemented in addition to the base
   275	[GitHub Flavored Markdown](https://github.github.com/gfm/) specification.
   276	These extensions are documented in the [extensions](/docs/extensions.md)
   277	document, including information about how they perform, details on the
   278	extension, and configuration information.

   301	## Open Issues and Future Plans
   302	
   303	During the development phase of this project, it was more useful to have
   304	an actual list of issues to track and prioritize, rather than relying on
   305	GitHub to do all the work. This is the location of the prioritized
   306	[Issues List](/issues.md).
   307	
   308	If you find any issues, please report them using the standard GitHub
   309	issues process.  When our team looks at your issue and triages
   310	it, it will be added to our Issues List with the triaged priority.
   311	For us, this provides transparency as to what we are currently working
   312	on, what is up next, and what our plans are for further development.

    def __rehydrate_block_quote(
        self, current_token, previous_token, next_token, transformed_data
    ):
        _ = (previous_token, transformed_data)

        new_instance = copy.deepcopy(current_token)
        new_instance.leading_text_index = 0
        self.container_token_stack.append(new_instance)
        print(f">bquote>{ParserHelper.make_value_visible(new_instance)}")
        print(
            f">self.container_token_stack>{ParserHelper.make_value_visible(self.container_token_stack)}"
        )

        token_stack_index = len(self.container_token_stack) - 2
        while (
            token_stack_index >= 0
            and self.container_token_stack[token_stack_index].is_block_quote_start
        ):
            token_stack_index -= 1
        print(f">token_stack_index>{token_stack_index}")

    def __rehydrate_block_quote_end(self, current_token, actual_tokens, token_index):

        _ = current_token
        print(f">>{ParserHelper.make_value_visible(actual_tokens[token_index:])}")
        search_index = token_index + 1
        while (
            search_index < len(actual_tokens)
            and actual_tokens[search_index].is_container_end_token
        ):
            search_index += 1
        print(f">>{search_index}")
        any_non_container_end_tokens = search_index < len(actual_tokens)
        print(f">>{any_non_container_end_tokens}")

        del self.container_token_stack[-1]

        return ""

    def __rehydrate_blank_line(self, current_token, previous_token):
        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        if (
            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text
        ):
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ParserHelper.newline_character
        else:
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ""

        return f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"

"""
Debug script to reproduce the issue with extra line in regenerated markdown.
"""

import sys
import os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '.'))

from test.utils import act_and_assert
from test.transform_to_markdown import TransformToMarkdown
from pymarkdown.tokenized_markdown import TokenizedMarkdown
from pymarkdown.transform_to_gfm import TransformToGfm

def debug_test():
    """Test the specific issue."""
    
    # Test case from the issue
    source_markdown = """   >    >    >
   >    >    > list"""
   
    print("Source markdown:")
    print(repr(source_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Tokenize
    tokenizer = TokenizedMarkdown()
    tokens = tokenizer.transform(source_markdown)
    
    print("Tokens:")
    for i, token in enumerate(tokens):
        print(f"{i:2d}: {token}")
    print()
    
    # Transform to markdown
    transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
    regenerated_markdown = transformer.transform(tokens)
    
    print("Regenerated markdown:")
    print(repr(regenerated_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Expected result
    expected_markdown = """   >    >    >
   >    >    > list"""
   
    print("Expected markdown:")
    print(repr(expected_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Check if they match
    if regenerated_markdown == expected_markdown:
        print("✓ PASS: No extra line found")
    else:
        print("✗ FAIL: Extra line detected!")
        print("Difference:")
        print(f"Expected: {repr(expected_markdown)}")
        print(f"Actual  : {repr(regenerated_markdown)}")
        
        # Show character by character difference
        min_len = min(len(expected_markdown), len(regenerated_markdown))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if expected_markdown[i] != regenerated_markdown[i]:
                print(f"Difference at position {i}: expected {repr(expected_markdown[i])} but got {repr(regenerated_markdown[i])}")
                break
        if len(expected_markdown) != len(regenerated_markdown):
            print(f"Length difference: expected {len(expected_markdown)} chars, got {len(regenerated_markdown)} chars")

def debug_test():
    """Test the specific issue."""
    
    # Test case from the issue
    source_markdown = """   >    >    >
   >    >    > list"""
   
    print("Source markdown:")
    print(repr(source_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Tokenize
    tokenizer = TokenizedMarkdown()
    tokens = tokenizer.transform(source_markdown)
    
    print("Tokens:")
    for i, token in enumerate(tokens):
        print(f"{i:2d}: {token}")
    print()
    
    # Transform to markdown
    transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
    regenerated_markdown = transformer.transform(tokens)
    
    print("Regenerated markdown:")
    print(repr(regenerated_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Expected result
    expected_markdown = """   >    >    >
   >    >    > list"""
   
    print("Expected markdown:")
    print(repr(expected_markdown))
    print()
    
    # Check if they match
    if regenerated_markdown == expected_markdown:
        print("✓ PASS: No extra line found")
    else:
        print("✗ FAIL: Extra line detected!")
        print("Difference:")
        print(f"Expected: {repr(expected_markdown)}")
        print(f"Actual  : {repr(regenerated_markdown)}")
        
        # Show character by character difference
        min_len = min(len(expected_markdown), len(regenerated_markdown))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if expected_markdown[i] != regenerated_markdown[i]:
                print(f"Difference at position {i}: expected {repr(expected_markdown[i])} but got {repr(regenerated_markdown[i])}")
                break
        if len(expected_markdown) != len(regenerated_markdown):
            print(f"Length difference: expected {len(expected_markdown)} chars, got {len(regenerated_markdown)} chars")

-->   >    >    >\n\n             > list<--

So the issue is that there's an extra `\n` being added somewhere. In my test case, it seems like the issue is slightly different - it's inserting a newline between the `>` and `list` instead of having double newlines.

def test_nested_three_block_max_block_max_block_max_empty():

```
 3: [BLANK(1,15):]
 4: [BLANK(1,15):]
 5: [end-block-quote:::True]
```

So there are two BLANK tokens at the same position. Then later there's:
```
post-h>new_data>\n
```

This suggests that when handling blank lines, an extra newline is being added. 

Looking at the __rehydrate_blank_line method in the transform_to_markdown.py file:

```python
def __rehydrate_blank_line(self, current_token, previous_token):
    """
    Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
    """
    if (
        self.block_stack
        and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
        and previous_token.is_text
    ):
        extra_newline_after_text_token = ParserHelper.newline_character
    else:
        extra_newline_after_text_token = ""

    return f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"
```

The issue is that this method always adds `ParserHelper.newline_character` at the end, regardless of whether there's already a newline from the previous processing.

However, looking at the debug output more carefully, I notice that there are TWO blank line tokens at the same position:
- [BLANK(1,15):]
- [BLANK(1,15):]

Each of these would add its own newline, resulting in double newlines.

But wait, let me look at the issue description again. The issue shows:
```
Expected
-->   >    >    >\n             > list<--
Actual  
-->   >    >    >\n\n             > list<--
```

This suggests that there should be one newline but two are being generated.

Looking at the token generation, it seems like the issue might be in how blank lines are being processed in the context of block quotes. When there are nested block quotes and a blank line, perhaps the transformation is adding too many newlines.

   849	    def __process_next_token(
   850	        self,
   851	        current_token,
   852	        previous_token,
   853	        next_token,

   869	            start_handler_fn = self.start_token_handlers[current_token.token_name]
   870	            new_data = start_handler_fn(current_token, previous_token)

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>So when a BlankLineMarkdownToken is encountered, it calls __rehydrate_blank_line. Looking at the debug output, I can see that there are TWO blank line tokens being processed:

```
post-h>new_data>\n
---
post-h>new_data>\n
```

This means that each blank line token is adding its own newline. But looking at the issue, it seems like there should only be one newline.

Let me check what the tokens actually contain. From the debug output:
```
 3: [BLANK(1,15):]
 4: [BLANK(1,15):]
```

Both blank line tokens have empty extracted_whitespace. So each one will generate `\n` (since the __rehydrate_blank_line method returns `f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"`).

The issue seems to be that we have two blank line tokens when we should probably have just one, or the processing should handle duplicate blank lines differently.

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the debug output and the issue, I can now clearly state the problem:

5.1 What the problem is:
In the transformation from tokens back to markdown, when there are multiple BLANK tokens at the same position within nested block quotes, each blank token adds its own newline character, resulting in extra blank lines in the regenerated markdown.

5.2 Where the problem is located:
The issue is in the __rehydrate_blank_line method in test/transform_to_markdown.py. This method always adds a newline character regardless of context:
```python
return f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"
```

5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
The test case has nested block quotes where there are two BLANK tokens at the same position (both [BLANK(1,15):]). Each of these tokens generates its own newline, causing the extra blank line.

5.4 Best practices to take into account:
- Need to be careful not to break other functionality that depends on blank line handling
- Should look at the context to determine if a blank line should actually generate output
- Need to understand when duplicate blank lines should be consolidated

5.5 How to fix the problem:
Looking at the debug output, I can see that the issue occurs when there are multiple blank lines in succession. The transformation should probably consolidate these or avoid adding extra newlines in certain contexts.

Looking more carefully at the debug trace:
- There are two blank line tokens: [BLANK(1,15):] and [BLANK(1,15):]
- Each one calls __rehydrate_blank_line and adds a newline
- The result is that we get two newlines where we should get one

The fix should probably check if we're in a context where multiple blank lines should be consolidated, or if we should avoid adding a newline when the previous token was also a blank line that added a newline.

        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.

        # Check if this blank line is within continuing block quotes and should not generate output
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start and
            hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and

    @pytest.mark.gfm
    def test_nested_three_block_max_block_max_block_max_empty():
        """
        Verify that a nesting of block quote, block quote, block quote, with
        the maximum number of spaces allowed, and no text on the first line, works properly.
        """

        # Arrange
        source_markdown = """   >    >    >
       >    >    > list"""
        expected_tokens = [
            "[block-quote(1,4):   :   > ]",
            "[block-quote(1,9)::   >    > ]",
            "[block-quote(1,14)::   >    >    >\n   >    >    > ]",
            "[BLANK(1,15):]",
            "[BLANK(1,15):]",
            "[para(2,16):]",
            "[text(2,16):list:]",
            "[end-para:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
        ]
        expected_gfm = """<blockquote>
    <blockquote>
    <blockquote>
    <p>list</p>
    </blockquote>
    </blockquote>
    </blockquote>"""

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I can see what's happening:

1. `leading_spaces = '   >    >    >\n   >    >    > '` - This contains two lines separated by a newline
2. `split_leading_spaces = ['   >    >    >', '   >    >    > ']` - Split correctly
3. `leading_text_index = 1` - We're looking at the second element
4. `split_leading_spaces[1] = '   >    >    > '` - This is NOT an empty string

So my condition is wrong. The issue isn't that the leading space is empty, it's that these blank lines shouldn't generate output in this context at all.

Let me think about this differently. The issue from the original problem description shows:

```
Expected
-->   >    >    >\n             > list<--
Actual  
-->   >    >    >\n\n             > list<--
```

So there should be one newline but two are generated. In my test case, I see:

```
Expected: '   >    >    >\n   >    >    > list'
Actual:   '   >    >    >\n   >    >    > \nlist'
```

The problem is that there's a newline inserted between `>` and `list` where there shouldn't be one.

Looking at the debug, the issue is that both blank lines are generating `\n`. Since there are two BLANK tokens at the same position, they're both trying to add a newline. But in the context of nested block quotes that span multiple lines, these blank lines are placeholders and shouldn't generate actual content.

Let me revise my approach. Instead of checking if the leading space is empty, I should check if we're in a context where blank lines shouldn't generate output:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/test/transform_to_markdown.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def __rehydrate_blank_line(self, current_token, previous_token):
        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line is within continuing block quotes and should not generate output
        # When we have nested block quotes spanning multiple lines, blank lines between them 
        # should not generate additional whitespace
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            # If we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces, and we're not at the end,
            # this blank line may be a placeholder
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                split_leading_spaces = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
                # If we still have more leading spaces to process, this blank line is likely a placeholder
                if (self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_text_index < len(split_leading_spaces) - 1):
                    return ""

        if (
            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text

    @pytest.mark.gfm
    def test_nested_three_block_max_block_max_block_max_empty():
        """
        Verify that a nesting of block quote, block quote, block quote, with
        the maximum number of spaces allowed, and no text on the first line, works properly.

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

>       assert (
            source_markdown == original_markdown
        ), f"Markdown strings are not equal.{diff_values}"

Let me think about this differently. Maybe the issue is that I need to look at the previous token or the context differently. 

Looking at the debug trace more carefully:
- The block quote token has `leading_spaces = '   >    >    >\n   >    >    > '`
- This splits to `['   >    >    >', '   >    >    > ']` (2 elements)
- But we have 2 BLANK tokens at the same position

The issue might be that when we have a block quote that spans multiple lines, the blank lines between those lines should not generate output.

Let me try a different approach. Instead of checking the leading_text_index, let me check if we're dealing with consecutive blank lines or if the blank line is within a multi-line block quote context:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/test/transform_to_markdown.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def __rehydrate_blank_line(self, current_token, previous_token):
        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line is within continuing block quotes and should not generate output
        # When we have nested block quotes spanning multiple lines, blank lines between them 
        # should not generate additional whitespace
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            # If we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces that contain newlines,
            # and we're processing blank lines within that context, suppress them
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces and
                ParserHelper.newline_character in self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                # Count how many newlines are in the leading spaces - this indicates multi-line block quotes
                newline_count = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.count(ParserHelper.newline_character)
                if newline_count > 0:
                    # For multi-line block quotes, suppress blank lines that come after the block quote start
                    # but before actual content
                    return ""

            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text
        ):
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ParserHelper.newline_character
        else:
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ""

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line corresponds to newlines within multi-line block quotes
        # and should not generate additional output
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):

            # check if this blank line represents one of those internal newlines

        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line is within continuing block quotes and should not generate output
        # When we have nested block quotes spanning multiple lines, blank lines between them 
        # should not generate additional whitespace
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            # If we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces that contain newlines,
            # and we're processing blank lines within that context, suppress them
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces and
                ParserHelper.newline_character in self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                # Count how many newlines are in the leading spaces - this indicates multi-line block quotes
                newline_count = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.count(ParserHelper.newline_character)

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

The problem is that by the time we process the blank lines, the `leading_text_index` has already been incremented to point to the second element (index 1), and we're comparing it against `len - 1 = 1`. Since `1 < 1` is False, neither blank line gets suppressed.

But we have TWO blank lines for ONE internal newline. That's the key insight!

The block quote has:
- `leading_spaces = '   >    >    >\n   >    >    > '` (one newline)
- This splits to `['   >    >    >', '   >    >    > ']` (2 elements)

But we have 2 BLANK tokens. This suggests that each BLANK token corresponds to processing the transition between leading space segments.

Let me think about this differently. Maybe the issue is that I need to count how many BLANK tokens I've seen for this particular block quote and suppress the excess ones.

Actually, let me step back and think about what should happen:
1. First line: `   >    >    >` 
2. Newline inside the block quote
3. Second line: `   >    >    > `
4. Content line: `list`

The BLANK tokens represent the newline transitions within the multi-line block quote structure. Since there's only one newline in the leading spaces, there should only be one blank line suppressed, not two.

But wait, I think I misunderstood the structure. Let me look at the debug output more carefully.

```

The problem is that I'm suppressing ALL blank lines now, even the ones that should separate content.

Let me revert to a simpler approach. Let me just suppress ONE blank line when we have multi-line block quotes:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/test/transform_to_markdown.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def __rehydrate_blank_line(self, current_token, previous_token):
        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line corresponds to newlines within multi-line block quotes
        # and should not generate additional output
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            # If we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces that contain newlines,
            # check if this blank line represents one of those internal newlines
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces and
                ParserHelper.newline_character in self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                
                # Count newlines in leading spaces - this tells us how many internal blank lines to suppress
                newline_count = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.count(ParserHelper.newline_character)
                
                # If we still have unprocessed leading text segments, suppress this blank line
                # We suppress up to newline_count blank lines
                if (hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count')):
                    suppressed_count = self._suppressed_blank_line_count
                else:
                    suppressed_count = 0
                    
                if suppressed_count < newline_count:
                    if not hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count'):
                        self._suppressed_blank_line_count = 0
                    self._suppressed_blank_line_count += 1
                    return ""

                # Reset counter when we're done with this block quote
                if self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_text_index >= newline_count:
                    if hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count'):
                        delattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count')

        if (
            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text
        ):
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ParserHelper.newline_character
        else:
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ""

        result = f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"

        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line corresponds to newlines within multi-line block quotes
        # and should not generate additional output
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            # If we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces that contain newlines,
            # check if this blank line represents one of those internal newlines
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces and
                ParserHelper.newline_character in self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                
                # Count newlines in leading spaces - this tells us how many internal blank lines to suppress
                newline_count = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.count(ParserHelper.newline_character)
                
                # If we still have unprocessed leading text segments, suppress this blank line
                # We suppress up to newline_count blank lines
                if (hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count')):
                    suppressed_count = self._suppressed_blank_line_count
                else:
                    suppressed_count = 0
                    
                if suppressed_count < newline_count:
                    if not hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count'):
                        self._suppressed_blank_line_count = 0
                    self._suppressed_blank_line_count += 1
                    return ""

                # Reset counter when we're done with this block quote
                if self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_text_index >= newline_count:
                    if hasattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count'):
                        delattr(self, '_suppressed_blank_line_count')

            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)

        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line is redundant in the context of multi-line block quotes
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):

        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """

        # and should not generate additional output
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):

            # check if this blank line represents one of those internal newlines
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and

                ParserHelper.newline_character in self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):
                
                # Count newlines in leading spaces - this tells us how many internal blank lines to suppress
                newline_count = self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces.count(ParserHelper.newline_character)
                
                # Use a counter specific to this block quote instance
                counter_key = id(self.container_token_stack[-1])
                if not hasattr(self, '_blank_line_counters'):
                    self._blank_line_counters = {}
                
                if counter_key not in self._blank_line_counters:
                    self._blank_line_counters[counter_key] = 0
                
                suppressed_count = self._blank_line_counters[counter_key]
                    
                if suppressed_count < newline_count:
                    self._blank_line_counters[counter_key] += 1
                    return ""

                # Clean up counter when we're done with this block quote
                if self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_text_index >= newline_count:
                    if counter_key in self._blank_line_counters:
                        del self._blank_line_counters[counter_key]

            self.block_stack

            and previous_token.is_text

    @pytest.mark.gfm
    def test_nested_three_block_max_block_max_block_max_empty():
        """
        Verify that a nesting of block quote, block quote, block quote, with
        the maximum number of spaces allowed, and no text on the first line, works properly.
        """

        # Arrange
        source_markdown = """   >    >    >
       >    >    > list"""
        expected_tokens = [
            "[block-quote(1,4):   :   > ]",
            "[block-quote(1,9)::   >    > ]",
            "[block-quote(1,14)::   >    >    >\n   >    >    > ]",
            "[BLANK(1,15):]",
            "[BLANK(1,15):]",
            "[para(2,16):]",
            "[text(2,16):list:]",
            "[end-para:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
            "[end-block-quote:::True]",
        ]
        expected_gfm = """<blockquote>
    <blockquote>
    <blockquote>
    <p>list</p>
    </blockquote>
    </blockquote>
    </blockquote>"""

        # Act & Assert
>       act_and_assert(source_markdown, expected_gfm, expected_tokens)

    def verify_markdown_roundtrip(source_markdown, actual_tokens):
        """
        Verify that we can use the information in the tokens to do a round trip back
        to the original Markdown that created the token.
        """

        if "\t" in source_markdown:
            new_source = []
            split_source = source_markdown.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)
            for next_line in split_source:
                if "\t" in next_line:
                    next_line = ParserHelper.detabify_string(next_line)
                new_source.append(next_line)
            source_markdown = ParserHelper.newline_character.join(new_source)

        transformer = TransformToMarkdown()
        original_markdown = transformer.transform(actual_tokens)

        print(
            "".join(
                [
                    "\n-=-=-\nExpected\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\nActual\n-=-=-\n-->",
                    ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                    "<--\n-=-=-\n",
                ]
            )
        )
        diff = difflib.ndiff(source_markdown, original_markdown)
        diff_values = "".join(
            [
                "\n-=-=-n",
                ParserHelper.newline_character.join(list(diff)),
                "\n-=-=-expected\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(source_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-actual\n-->",
                ParserHelper.make_value_visible(original_markdown),
                "<--\n-=-=-\n",
            ]
        )

        """
        Rehydrate the blank line from the token.
        """
        # Check if this blank line is redundant in the context of multi-line block quotes
        if (self.container_token_stack and 
            self.container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start):
            
            # Check if we have a block quote with multiple leading spaces containing newlines
            if (hasattr(self.container_token_stack[-1], 'leading_spaces') and
                self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_spaces):

                # Only apply this logic if there are newlines in the leading spaces
                if ParserHelper.newline_character in leading_spaces:

                    split_spaces = leading_spaces.split(ParserHelper.newline_character)

                    # then it's likely a redundant blank line from the multi-line structure
                    if (len(split_spaces) > 1 and 
                        self.container_token_stack[-1].leading_text_index < len(split_spaces) - 1 and
                        not current_token.extracted_whitespace):
                        return ""

        if (
            self.block_stack
            and self.block_stack[-1].is_fenced_code_block
            and previous_token.is_text
        ):
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ParserHelper.newline_character
        else:
            extra_newline_after_text_token = ""

        result = f"{extra_newline_after_text_token}{current_token.extracted_whitespace}{ParserHelper.newline_character}"
        return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/test/transform_to_markdown.py has been edited.
